Falsification Criteria and Research Sequence
The programme separates architectural invariants from provisional mechanisms. Invariants are commitments whose removal would produce a different project: one regular outer sentence shape; explicit named roles; stable semantic node identity; proposition separated from commitment; visible context and provenance; deterministic validation before reasoning; profile-declared semantics; preserved alternatives where consequence changes; and constrained realisation. Even these can be falsified in principle, but they define what the current research is trying to test.
The open verb architecture is provisional in its details. The hypothesis is that semantic operators should be first-class, namespaced, typed, and versioned. The number and granularity of operators are empirical. Corpus studies may show that some distinctions belong as parameter values, type refinements, or rendering conventions rather than verbs. Confusion matrices, definition burden, library fragmentation, and migration cost determine whether verbs are merged or split.
The visible SSA syntax is also provisional. Explicit identifiers may improve diffing, caching, provenance, and review, or they may burden readers without sufficient gain. A comparison should test user-visible IDs against compiler-generated IDs, parenthesised frames, indentation, and structured editors. The canonical IR may retain SSA even if the default human view hides it.
Folder-derived namespaces are a pragmatic hypothesis. They make package structure visible and align semantic work with version control, but they impose one primary hierarchy and create refactoring risks. Manifest-only names, content-addressed modules, URIs, and federation schemes should be tested. Stable semantic identity must not depend accidentally on a file move.
The initial reasoning profile is provisional. Horn-style relational rules with explicit negation and four-valued status are attractive for a bounded core, but early use cases may require defaults, temporal reasoning, constraints, or probabilistic models sooner. The source language should remain stable while backends evolve. No backend earns universal status through implementation convenience.
RDF interoperability provides a direct baseline for the representation layer. Comparative experiments can test whether Semantic Web tooling offers equivalent authoring locality, execution, traceability, and governance with lower complexity. The same method applies to Datalog, ASP, JSON-based schemas, frame CNLs, graph models, and direct LLM tool use. Results determine whether SLEnglish is best used as a primary source language, a thin authoring notation, or a specialised interchange layer.
A decisive falsification criterion is failure to reduce false assurance or review cost relative to strong baselines. Additional criteria are semantic fragmentation across libraries, poor translation fidelity, excessive authoring cost, unstable operator semantics, and inability to preserve provenance across backend conversions. These criteria provide concrete reasons to simplify, replace, merge, or retire language features as evidence accumulates.
Reversibility requires migration tools and frozen corpora. Every release has a conformance suite and canonical examples. Deprecation retains old readers. Bridges state whether translations are equivalent, approximate, or lossy. Published scientific packages remain executable under their locked environment. The language can evolve without rewriting history.
Design decision
How it can change without invalidating the whole programme
Visible SSA identities
Compare visible IDs, editor-generated IDs, hidden canonical IDs, frames, and indentation while retaining stable internal identity.
`$` and `~` reference distinction
Test alternative sigils or typed references; preserve the semantic difference between value flow and node reference.
Arbitrary-depth path namespaces
Compare folder paths with manifest names, content-addressed identities, URI aliases, and federated registries. File moves must not silently change semantic identity.
One operator per semantic distinction
Merge, split, or demote candidates to parameters, enum values, types, or rendering conventions based on corpus evidence and correction cost.
Common parameter registry
Promote only stable cross-domain roles; retain path-qualified local parameters where generalisation would erase meaning.
LLM-first translation
Compare direct expert authoring, grammar-based parsers, encoders, hybrid compilers, and multiple model families.
Interpreter portfolio
Add or remove backends according to task fit, semantic preservation, complexity, cost, and certificate quality.
Four-valued status
Compare paraconsistent, three-valued, provenance-weighted, and profile-specific conflict semantics.
Canonical text surface
Retain the source representation only if it improves review, diffing, portability, and reproducibility relative to structured editors or binary IRs.
Failure condition
Required response
No reduction in false assurance
Narrow or abandon the language layer even if answer accuracy or demonstration quality appears high.
Correction cost exceeds baselines
Simplify the surface, improve tooling, reduce operator granularity, or restrict use to machine-to-machine exchange.
Semantic library fragmentation
Strengthen governance and bridge modules; if interoperability remains poor, prefer established domain formalisms.
Backend-specific leakage dominates
Allow profile-specific syntax or use direct formal languages rather than preserving a misleading common envelope.
Path organisation proves unstable
Move semantic identity to manifests or content addressing while retaining readable aliases.
Round-trip renderings strengthen claims
Restrict generation, improve licensing checks, or require human-authored publication prose.
Security or authority boundaries remain unclear
Prohibit operational effects and retain SLEnglish as a declarative audit representation.
Existing standards perform equivalently at lower cost
Use SLEnglish only as a thin view or discontinue it.
No domain shows durable value
Publish the negative result and stop standardisation rather than expanding the vocabulary.
Research phase
Bounded outcome and decision
Reference kernel
Implement independent parsers, path resolution, schemas, SSA references, contexts, canonicalisation, source spans, strict rules, queries, and proof-linked diagnostics.
Linguistic and scientific profiles
Test evidence, time, identity, collective agency, causation, units, uncertainty, coverage, and English-construction libraries on curated corpora.
Interpreter adapters
Implement a small set of serious lowerings, document semantic preservation, and compare query-first with broader materialisation.
Human compiler tools
Evaluate natural-language translation, alternatives, local correction, semantic diff, controlled rendering, and expert authoring.
Executable research object
Create one bounded package containing prose, data, code, claims, reviews, revisions, queries, and independent reproduction.
External replication and governance
Require another implementation or team, held-out domains, migration evidence, issue records, and documented failures before stabilising a public standard.
A research corpus should accompany the implementation from the beginning. It should contain ordinary English constructions, typologically motivated distinctions, scientific claims, contradictory reports, incomplete coverage, identity ambiguity, collective attribution, temporal updates, normative rules, and adversarial cases. Every revision of an operator contract runs against the frozen corpus. New cases are added with an explanation of the failure they expose.
Independent implementation is especially important because a language can appear coherent when parser, compiler, runtime, benchmark, and renderer share the same assumptions. Two parsers should agree on canonical structure. Independent adapters should agree where semantic preservation is claimed. A third party should be able to modify a research package and predict which conclusions require rebuilding. Disagreement is diagnostic evidence rather than an inconvenience to hide.
Negative results and migration records are part of the research output. A candidate operator that confuses reviewers, an ontology bridge that loses meaning, or a backend that cannot preserve provenance identifies a design boundary. Conformance tests and migration tools then convert those failures into changes that can be compared on the same corpus.
Conclusion
Natural language is the main medium in which scientific questions, explanations, disagreements, and institutional decisions are expressed. Its economy leaves source, identity, scope, coverage, authority, time, and alternative interpretations implicit when context is sufficient for human readers. Computational use requires those variables to become explicit at the points where they change denotation, inference, or action.
SLEnglish provides that explicit boundary. English grammar supplies the baseline structures to normalise; cross-linguistic research supplies additional semantic distinctions; philosophy and systems research refine identity, agency, causation, boundaries, uncertainty, and collective concepts. The resulting distinctions are represented as path-addressed verbs with named parameters, stable SSA identities, contexts, evidence links, rules, and queries. The operator and parameter tables in the appendices are the concrete synthesis of this analysis.
Natural-language translation can be produced by an LLM, a grammar, an expert, or a hybrid compiler and then checked by deterministic parsers, schemas, type systems, source anchors, and semantic tests. Execution can use rules, graphs, ontologies, solvers, temporal models, theorem provers, generated code, simulations, semantic circuits, and specialised scientific tools. Adapters state the fragment they support and the guarantees they preserve.
Executable science emerges when selected claims, evidence, assumptions, transformations, and dependencies can answer bounded questions, expose contradictions, support review, and respond predictably to correction. SLEnglish acts as the semantic source layer that connects these objects while keeping their interpretation and provenance inspectable.
The programme evaluates SLEnglish by translation fidelity, source traceability, false-assurance rate, correction cost, review effort, interoperability, and execution behaviour. Syntax, operator granularity, parameter registries, namespace conventions, and backend mappings remain measurable design variables. The operator library becomes progressively smaller, clearer, and more useful as comparative experiments identify which distinctions consistently improve representation and reasoning.
APPENDIX A
Core Grammar and Canonicalisation
This appendix states the candidate source-level grammar independently of any runtime. The grammar is intentionally small. Operator files and profiles supply semantics; the parser supplies only a deterministic envelope and explicit references.
Grammar element
Normative description
Document
A sequence of declarations and comments encoded in UTF-8. Canonical source contains only declarations; comments and editor metadata do not affect semantic hashes unless explicitly included by a profile.
Declaration
`@ssa-id operator-path parameter value parameter value ... .` One declaration creates one SSA node.
SSA identifier
`@` followed by a lowercase kebab-case identifier unique within the declared source scope. Revision creates a new identifier or epoch rather than mutation.
Operator path
A relative slash-separated path of arbitrary depth ending in `.sle`, such as `knowledge/evidence/report.sle`. Resolution uses root manifests, imports, versions, and content digests.
Parameter
A declared role name followed by one value. The operator contract defines legality, type, cardinality, repetition, conditions, and canonical order.
Value reference
`$name` requests the primary value produced by an earlier node and creates a data dependency.
Node reference
`~name` refers to the semantic node itself and preserves proposition identity, provenance, alternatives, and status.
Atomic value
A declared lowercase symbol, boolean, canonical number, date-time, unit, enum value, context, entity identifier, or registered datatype literal.
Quoted value
A UTF-8 string delimited by double quotation marks with defined escaping. Language, datatype, or source role is stated by parameters or contract.
Missing-value atom
A declared semantic atom such as `unknown`, `not-applicable`, `withheld`, `not-measured`, `conflicted`, or `open-end`; omission remains distinct.
Terminator
A final period separated by whitespace from the preceding value. The period in `.sle` belongs to the operator path and is not a statement terminator.
Physical line
A declaration may be displayed on one or several physical lines. Canonical exchange uses one normalised token stream per declaration.
Comment
An editor or source profile may support comments, but comments do not introduce executable meaning unless materialised through a declared annotation operator.
Canonicalisation rule
Required behaviour
Whitespace
Use one ASCII space between canonical tokens; normalise line endings; remove non-semantic indentation and trailing whitespace.
Identifiers
Use lowercase kebab-case for local machine identities. Human-readable and multilingual names remain labels.
Operator paths
Resolve root aliases, normalise relative path segments, prohibit ambiguous traversal, and record package digest plus file version. Arbitrary directory depth is allowed.
Parameter order
Serialise parameters in the order declared by the operator contract. Repeated unordered roles sort by canonical identity; ordered roles preserve semantic order.
Numbers and dates
Use locale-independent canonical forms and declared datatypes. Physical quantities retain a separate unit value.
Booleans and missing values
Use only registered canonical atoms; do not equate omission, false, zero, empty, unknown, or not applicable.
Aliases
Resolve aliases before semantic hashing while retaining the authoring alias in source metadata.
Canonicalisation rule
Required behaviour
Versions
Pin imported roots and external schemas through a lock record. A path without a resolved version is not publication-canonical.
Revision
Do not mutate nodes in place. Record supersession, derivation, and source-revision lineage.
Effects
Declarative interpretation is the default. Any external effect requires a separately authorised operational profile and an execution receipt.
Unknown operators
Reject or retain as explicitly opaque according to policy. Never infer a contract from the spelling alone.
Hash scope
Hash the resolved operator identity, canonical parameters and values, selected context/profile identifiers, and any contract-declared semantic metadata.
document = declaration* declaration = binding SP operator-path (SP parameter SP value)* SP "." binding = "@" identifier operator-path = path-segment ("/" path-segment)* ".sle" parameter = identifier | qualified-parameter value = atom | string | number | boolean | missing | value-ref | node-ref value-ref = "$" identifier node-ref = "~" identifier
APPENDIX B
Common Parameter Registry
The registry collects parameter names that recur across linguistic, epistemic, scientific, social, temporal, and systemic operators. A common parameter is retained when its semantics are stable enough for type checking and interoperability; specialised roles use path-qualified names. The table provides the shared vocabulary used by the operator contracts in the following appendices.
Term
Explanation
id
Stable local or qualified identity of an entity, node, artefact, or external record.
name
Canonical symbolic name. Labels for readers remain separate and may be multilingual.
label
Human-readable label; never used as the sole machine identity.
description
Natural-language documentation that is informative but not executable unless compiled separately.
type
Declared semantic or data type of a value, entity, proposition, operator, or result.
parent
Immediate supertype, parent context, parent module, or parent artefact as defined by the verb schema.
module
Qualified package module that owns or supplies the referenced object.
version
Semantic version, immutable release identifier, or external artefact version.
namespace
Readable root or relative operator path used for resolution. Canonical semantic identity also records the package digest, file version, and exported contract.
profile
Named reasoning, validation, evidence, identity, scientific, or operational profile.
context
World, discourse, institutional, quoted, hypothetical, or execution context in which the node applies.
world
Explicit possible, actual, fictional, simulated, counterfactual, or reported world.
jurisdiction
Legal, policy, organisational, or community authority under which a rule or claim applies.
language
Human language or controlled-language variant used by labels, source material, or rendering.
source
Evidence source, originating entity, starting point of a relation, or source artefact according to schema.
source-span
Exact source location supporting or giving rise to the node.
source-depth
Number and structure of testimonial or derivational hops between claim and primary source.
content-digest
Cryptographic or content-addressed digest of a canonical artefact.
valid-time
Interval during which the represented fact, rule, state, licence, or authority is valid.
transaction-time
Interval during which the record exists in the knowledge system.
time
Instant, interval, relative temporal expression, or temporal node associated with the proposition.
start
Beginning instant or boundary of an interval, process, validity period, or action.
end
Ending instant or boundary; `open-end` may denote an intentionally unclosed interval.
duration
Measured or symbolic length of an event, process, state, or validity period.
frequency
Rate or count of repetitions under an explicit temporal or event scope.
interval
Named temporal, numeric, spatial, or uncertainty interval.
scale
Micro, meso, macro, spatial, temporal, organisational, or measurement scale at which a claim is made.
agent
Intentional or conventionally acting participant responsible for an event role.
actor
Participant that performs a role without presupposing full intention or moral agency.
causer
Participant, event, condition, or structure occupying a causal source role.
causee
Participant caused or enabled to act by a causative construction.
patient
Participant undergoing an event or being materially affected.
theme
Participant moved, transferred, perceived, communicated, or otherwise central to an event.
recipient
Participant receiving an object, information, role, permission, or effect.
beneficiary
Participant for whose benefit or detriment an event is performed.
experiencer
Participant undergoing a perception, emotion, cognition, or internal state.
stimulus
Entity or event giving rise to an experience or perception.
instrument
Tool, method, channel, or artefact used to perform an event.
origin
Initial place, owner, state, version, or source endpoint.
destination
Final place, owner, state, version, or target endpoint.
path
Ordered route, derivation chain, causal chain, or transition sequence.
location
Spatial, virtual, organisational, or document location.
possessor
Entity participating in a possession, control, stewardship, kin, or inalienable relation.
possessed
Entity, role, resource, body part, right, or relation associated with a possessor.
whole
Entity treated as the whole in a mereological relation.
part
Component, portion, member, ingredient, phase, or layer related to a whole.
member
Individual or subcollective participating in a collective.
collective
Group, swarm, organisation, institution, population, or aggregate.
speaker
Current discourse producer or quoted speaker.
addressee
Current discourse recipient or quoted addressee.
holder
Agent holding a belief, obligation, permission, role, right, attitude, or perspective.
anchor
Reference point for spatial, temporal, discourse, social, or perspectival interpretation.
perspective
Explicit viewpoint, logophoric centre, information state, or evaluative frame.
source-agent
Person, organisation, model, sensor, or process responsible for producing evidence.
target
Endpoint, compared entity, affected proposition, goal, or object selected by the verb schema.
proposition
Reference to semantic content without necessarily committing to its truth.
premise
Proposition used as an input to a derivation, argument, or analysis.
conclusion
Proposition produced or supported by a derivation, argument, or analysis.
pattern
Proposition template, graph pattern, event pattern, or constraint pattern containing variables.
basis
Reason, criterion, model, observation, rule, definition, or similarity dimension supporting a relation.
mode
Verb-specific semantic subtype when separate qualified verbs would be unnecessarily granular.
polarity
Positive or explicit negative status; omission never means negative.
degree
Scalar, ordinal, fuzzy, or graded strength under a declared scale.
strength
Causal, evidential, modal, normative, or statistical strength as defined by a profile.
confidence
Confidence in an estimate, mapping, claim, source, or compilation—not the event probability itself.
probability
Probability assigned to an event, hypothesis, outcome, or model under an explicit interpretation.
uncertainty
Uncertainty value or structured uncertainty object, including measurement and model uncertainty.
unit
Declared unit of measurement; required by scientific profiles for physical quantities.
value
Literal, symbol, entity, collection, or computed output carried by the node.
from
Initial value or state in a change relation.
to
Final value or state in a change relation.
min
Lower numeric or ordered bound.
max
Upper numeric or ordered bound.
threshold
Boundary whose approach or crossing changes status, behaviour, obligation, or phase.
classifier
Counting or ontological classifier applied to a quantity or referent.
cardinality
Exact, minimum, maximum, interval, or named number category.
quantity
Count, magnitude, ratio, amount, or quantity object.
distribution
Statistical, spatial, temporal, responsibility, or participant distribution.
aspect
Internal temporal contour such as perfective, imperfective, progressive, habitual, telic, or iterative.
tense
Temporal relation between event time, reference time, and speech or evaluation time.
remoteness
Distance category such as immediate, recent, remote, historical, near-future, or remote-future.
realis-status
Actual, possible, intended, hypothetical, counterfactual, or otherwise irrealis status.
evidence-mode
Observed, sensed, measured, reported, inferred, remembered, simulated, generated, or unknown source mode.
mirativity
Expected, unexpected, counterexpected, surprising, or model-revising information status.
identity-mode
Strict, material, structural, processual, causal, genealogical, informational, functional, role, or legal continuity.
agency-mode
Individual, delegated, distributed, emergent, centralised, coordinated, or fictionalised agency.
decision-mode
Vote, command, consensus, market outcome, delegated decision, algorithmic selection, or other decision architecture.
causal-layer
Immediate, contributory, enabling, structural, mediated, preventive, triggering, or feedback causal level.
reversibility
Reversible, costly-to-reverse, practically irreversible, or physically irreversible status.
effect-order
Direct, second-order, third-order, cascade, or recursively propagated consequence.
feedback
Positive, negative, delayed, stabilising, destabilising, or mixed feedback regime.
boundary
Sharp, fuzzy, operational, disputed, dynamic, permeable, or scale-dependent boundary status.
mandate
Rule, vote, delegation, charter, consent, or authority that licences representation or action.
coordination
None, local, stigmergic, negotiated, centralised, distributed, synchronised, or emergent coordination.
control
Capacity to select, prevent, redirect, repair, or authorise an outcome.
responsibility
Typed allocation of causal, role, control, knowledge, benefit, or remedial responsibility.
owner
Entity governing a module, artefact, resource, definition, or right.
authority
Institution, person, standard, contract, or capability that validates or permits an action.
method
Measurement, analysis, inference, retrieval, experimental, or compilation method.
dataset
Versioned dataset, table, stream, corpus, or data slice used or produced.
model
Scientific, statistical, causal, simulation, machine-learning, or semantic model.
code
Versioned executable artefact or source module referenced by a scientific or operational node.
environment
Container, hardware, library lock, solver configuration, or execution environment.
query
Reference to the query for which a result, proof, diagnostic, or explanation is produced.
return
Variable, field, proposition, proof component, or aggregate requested by a query.
order
Deterministic ordering criterion for query or execution output.
limit
Result, time, memory, search, token, or iteration limit.
witness
Replayable example, model, path, trace, or counterexample supporting an execution result.
proof
Proof object, certificate, derivation, or checkable receipt.
status
Accepted, proposed, derived, rejected, unknown, conflicted, superseded, or other governed state.
coverage
Scope, completeness policy, revision, exclusions, and method supporting positive or negative conclusions.
missing-reason
Unknown, not measured, withheld, not applicable, unsupported, unavailable, or conflicted reason.
capability
Explicit permission required for an operational interpreter or external effect.
budget
Time, memory, cost, search, token, energy, or external-service allowance.
determinism
Deterministic, seeded, stochastic, nondeterministic, or externally contingent execution contract.
monotonicity
Monotonic, antimonotonic, non-monotonic, unknown, or context-dependent semantic behaviour.
effect
Pure, state-read, state-write, file, network, tool, notification, or other operational effect class.
cache
Cacheability and invalidation contract for a node or operator.
severity
Informational, warning, error, blocking, or domain-specific priority of a diagnostic or constraint.
priority
Rule, default, scheduling, review, or conflict-resolution priority.
when
Premise or condition that activates a rule, constraint, decision, or query.
unless
Exception or defeater that blocks a default, rule, constraint, or action.
then
Conclusion, effect, or output activated by a rule.
alternative
Materially distinct semantic candidate retained without premature selection.
assumption
Explicit premise introduced by a model, human, method, or profile rather than directly supported by the source.
alias
Local import or symbol alias resolved to a qualified canonical identity before hashing.
guarantee
Declared guarantee envelope such as syntactic, source-grounded, model-relative, bounded, proved, or replay-verified.
output-type
Declared type of the value or semantic node produced by a registered verb.
required
List or schema object describing parameters that every use of a verb must provide.
optional
List or schema object describing parameters that a verb may accept without changing its identity.
replacement
New node, value, rule, or commitment that supersedes an earlier target.
variable
Typed logical or query variable introduced for binding in patterns and rules.
cause
Event, agent, condition, or proposition presented as the cause of a change under a declared causal profile.
purpose
Goal or intended function attributed to an action without conflating intention with achieved effect.
relation
Declared relation symbol or relation node used as the content of a higher-order construction.
role
Named semantic, social, institutional, discourse, or provenance role.
rule
Registered rule or derivation object used to license an inference.
alternatives
Collection of materially distinct semantic candidates retained for comparison or suspended judgement.
politeness
Register, deference, familiarity, face-management, or conventional politeness value.
distance
Spatial, temporal, social, causal, epistemic, or threshold-relative distance under a named scale.
rank
Social, institutional, taxonomic, preference, or result rank under an explicit ordering.
relationship
Typed interpersonal, kin, institutional, contractual, or discourse relationship.
community
Community, speech group, institution, or governance body relative to which a convention applies.
direction
Absolute, intrinsic, relative, temporal, causal, or scalar direction.
process
Process node or process description relevant to continuation, unboundedness, or transformation.
intention
Declared intention, plan, or goal state of an agent, kept distinct from effect and responsibility.
mechanism
Causal, operational, biological, social, or computational mechanism linking source and target.
delay
Temporal lag between cause and effect, signal and response, or action and consequence.
incentive
Reward, penalty, target, metric, or institutional pressure capable of changing behaviour.
result
Result artefact or outcome produced, compared, replicated, or consumed by another node.
APPENDIX C
Candidate Operator Library
The table is the main implementation output of the analysis in the book. It combines ordinary semantic operations with operators motivated by English grammar, cross-linguistic constructions, epistemology, scientific practice, social organisation, and systems reasoning. Each retained path corresponds to a `.sle` contract defining parameters, types, semantic conditions, examples, tests, and compatible interpreters.
Term
Explanation
slenglish/core/import.sle
Loads a versioned module into the current package and binds an optional local alias. Required: module version. Optional: namespace alias content-digest profile. Output: module handle.
slenglish/core/define-verb.sle
Declares a typed semantic operator and its executable contract. Required: name output-type. Optional: module version required optional cardinality determinism monotonicity effect profile description. Output: verb schema.
slenglish/core/define-type.sle
Declares a semantic or data type. Required: name. Optional: parent module version description. Output: type handle.
slenglish/core/define-role.sle
Declares a reusable parameter or semantic role. Required: name. Optional: type cardinality description module version. Output: role handle.
slenglish/core/define-enum.sle
Declares a closed or versioned set of symbolic values. Required: name value. Optional: description module version. Output: enum handle.
slenglish/core/define-unit.sle
Declares a unit, dimension, conversion relation, and authority. Required: name. Optional: type basis authority version description. Output: unit handle.
slenglish/core/alias.sle
Binds a local readable name to a stable qualified symbol. Required: name target. Optional: context version. Output: alias handle.
slenglish/core/entity.sle
Creates or registers a typed entity without asserting further contingent facts. Required: type. Optional: id name label description source content-digest context. Output: entity value.
slenglish/core/variable.sle
Creates a typed variable for patterns, rules, and queries. Required: type. Optional: name description context. Output: variable value.
slenglish/core/context.sle
Creates a world, discourse, legal, quoted, hypothetical, or execution context. Required: name. Optional: parent world jurisdiction profile valid-time source coverage description. Output: context handle.
slenglish/core/assert.sle
Commits a proposition as accepted in a named context. Required: proposition context. Optional: source source-span valid-time authority status. Output: commitment.
slenglish/core/deny.sle
Commits explicit negative polarity for a proposition in a named context. Required: proposition context. Optional: source source-span valid-time authority status. Output: negative commitment.
slenglish/core/claim.sle
Records that a source or agent advances a proposition without automatically accepting it. Required: proposition source. Optional: source-span source-depth evidence-mode confidence valid-time context status. Output: claim.
slenglish/core/hypothesize.sle
Places a proposition in a hypothesis context for testing rather than acceptance. Required: proposition context. Optional: source source-span confidence assumption profile status. Output: hypothesis commitment.
slenglish/core/assume.sle
Introduces an explicit premise for a bounded analysis. Required: proposition context. Optional: source basis valid-time status. Output: assumption commitment.
slenglish/core/quote.sle
Records the exact or controlled content attributed to a source or speaker. Required: proposition source. Optional: source-span speaker addressee time context language. Output: quotation.
slenglish/core/retract.sle
Withdraws a prior commitment while preserving historical provenance. Required: target. Optional: source basis time authority context. Output: retraction.
slenglish/core/supersede.sle
Declares that a new node replaces an earlier node for specified uses. Required: target replacement. Optional: basis time context version. Output: supersession relation.
slenglish/core/rule.sle
Declares a strict, default, causal, temporal, probabilistic, or profile-specific derivation. Required: when then profile. Optional: unless priority context source description. Output: rule handle.
slenglish/core/constraint.sle
Declares a validation, consistency, cardinality, numeric, temporal, or policy obligation. Required: when profile. Optional: unless severity context source description. Output: constraint handle.
slenglish/core/query.sle
Creates a first-class query with explicit context, guarantee, coverage, and return contract. Required: pattern return. Optional: context profile coverage guarantee order limit budget description. Output: query handle.
slenglish/core/invoke.sle
Requests execution of a registered operational circuit under explicit capability. Required: target capability. Optional: value context budget environment effect source. Output: invocation request.
slenglish/core/explain.sle
Requests a source-linked explanation of a result, proof, conflict, or dependency. Required: target. Optional: query profile limit language perspective. Output: explanation request.
slenglish/core/diagnose.sle
Creates a typed diagnostic for ambiguity, loss, invalidity, conflict, or unsupported semantics. Required: target status. Optional: source-span severity basis alternative description. Output: diagnostic.
knowledge/logic/and.sle
Constructs a conjunction whose operands must be interpreted under the same named profile. Required: proposition proposition. Optional: context order. Output: conjunctive proposition.
knowledge/logic/or.sle
Constructs an inclusive disjunction unless a profile declares another mode. Required: proposition proposition. Optional: context mode order. Output: disjunctive proposition.
knowledge/logic/not.sle
Constructs explicit logical negation rather than absence or failure to derive. Required: proposition. Optional: context profile. Output: negative proposition.
knowledge/logic/imply.sle
Constructs material, strict, or profile-specific implication without itself asserting either side. Required: premise conclusion. Optional: profile context. Output: implication proposition.
knowledge/logic/equivalent.sle
Declares equivalence under a named semantics and scope. Required: source target. Optional: profile context basis strength. Output: equivalence proposition.
knowledge/logic/each.sle
Binds universal quantification over a typed domain. Required: variable type. Optional: when context profile. Output: quantified proposition.
knowledge/logic/some.sle
Binds existential quantification over a typed domain. Required: variable type. Optional: when context profile. Output: quantified proposition.
knowledge/logic/none.sle
States that no member of a typed, covered domain satisfies a pattern. Required: variable type pattern. Optional: coverage context profile. Output: negative quantified proposition.
knowledge/logic/exactly.sle
Constrains the number of matches to an exact cardinality. Required: pattern cardinality. Optional: coverage context profile. Output: cardinality constraint.
knowledge/logic/at-least.sle
Constrains the number or degree of matches to a lower bound. Required: pattern min. Optional: coverage context profile unit. Output: lower-bound constraint.
knowledge/logic/at-most.sle
Constrains the number or degree of matches to an upper bound. Required: pattern max. Optional: coverage context profile unit. Output: upper-bound constraint.
knowledge/entity/classify.sle
Places an entity in a type without claiming strict identity with the type. Required: source type. Optional: context valid-time basis source-span. Output: classification proposition.
knowledge/entity/exist.sle
States existence in an explicit world, interval, or model. Required: source context. Optional: world valid-time evidence-mode source-span. Output: existence proposition.
knowledge/entity/equal.sle
States strict substitutive identity under an explicitly named profile. Required: source target. Optional: context basis valid-time. Output: identity proposition.
knowledge/entity/different.sle
States non-identity under an explicitly named profile. Required: source target. Optional: context basis valid-time. Output: difference proposition.
knowledge/entity/name.sle
Associates an entity with a label, identifier, or name in a language and scope. Required: source label. Optional: language context valid-time authority. Output: naming proposition.
knowledge/property/state.sle
Attributes a typed state or property value to an entity. Required: source value. Optional: type unit valid-time context basis. Output: state proposition.
knowledge/property/change.sle
Represents change of a property between explicit states or values. Required: source from to. Optional: time duration cause context. Output: change proposition.
knowledge/relation/relate.sle
Constructs a declared relation when no more specialised verb is available. Required: source target type. Optional: context valid-time basis degree. Output: relation proposition.
knowledge/relation/compare.sle
Compares two values or entities along an explicit dimension and comparator. Required: source target basis. Optional: mode value unit context. Output: comparison proposition.
knowledge/relation/depend-on.sle
Represents semantic, causal, build, resource, or logical dependency. Required: source target. Optional: mode strength context valid-time. Output: dependency proposition.
knowledge/relation/associate.sle
Represents an explicitly non-causal association with method and strength. Required: source target. Optional: method basis strength confidence context dataset. Output: association proposition.
language/event/occur.sle
Creates an event with explicit participants, time, and context. Required: type. Optional: agent actor patient theme location time duration context source-span. Output: event proposition.
language/event/act.sle
Represents an action by an agent or actor toward an optional target. Required: agent. Optional: target patient theme instrument purpose time location context. Output: action proposition.
language/event/transfer.sle
Represents transfer of a theme between origin and destination or recipient. Required: theme. Optional: agent origin destination recipient instrument time purpose context. Output: transfer event.
language/event/move.sle
Represents spatial, virtual, organisational, or state-space movement. Required: theme origin destination. Optional: agent path instrument time duration context. Output: motion event.
language/event/create.sle
Represents bringing an entity, record, artefact, or state into existence. Required: agent target. Optional: instrument method time context source. Output: creation event.
language/event/destroy.sle
Represents termination or destruction with explicit patient and causal context. Required: agent patient. Optional: instrument method time context reversibility. Output: destruction event.
language/event/communicate.sle
Represents transmission of semantic content between agents or systems. Required: source-agent recipient proposition. Optional: instrument language time context evidence-mode. Output: communication event.
language/event/perceive.sle
Represents perception by an experiencer through an optional sense or instrument. Required: experiencer stimulus. Optional: instrument mode time location context. Output: perception event.
language/event/experience.sle
Represents an internal state or experience without imposing external observability. Required: experiencer value. Optional: stimulus degree time context. Output: experience event.
language/event/change.sle
Represents a transition of an entity, relation, or system between states. Required: target from to. Optional: causer time duration reversibility context. Output: change event.
language/event/repeat.sle
Represents repeated realisations of an event under frequency and distribution. Required: proposition frequency. Optional: interval distribution context. Output: pluractional event.
language/event/distribute.sle
Distributes an action or relation across members, times, or objects. Required: proposition distribution. Optional: collective member interval context. Output: distributed event.
language/event/reciprocate.sle
Represents mutually directed instances of a relation among participants. Required: relation collective. Optional: member time context. Output: reciprocal proposition.
language/event/reflexive.sle
Represents a participant filling two co-referential semantic roles. Required: agent. Optional: patient theme role context. Output: reflexive event.
language/event/causative.sle
Adds an explicit causer and causee to an event structure. Required: causer proposition. Optional: causee instrument causal-layer time context. Output: causative event.
language/event/applicative.sle
Promotes a beneficiary, instrument, location, or recipient into a core event role. Required: proposition target mode. Optional: context. Output: applicative event.
language/event/antipassive.sle
Suppresses or backgrounds a patient while foregrounding an agent and activity. Required: proposition agent. Optional: patient aspect context. Output: antipassive event.
language/event/middle.sle
Represents an event whose affected participant is central without a fully external agent. Required: patient proposition. Optional: agent aspect context. Output: middle event.
language/event/passive.sle
Represents a valency view that backgrounds or omits the agent while retaining event identity. Required: proposition patient. Optional: agent context evidence-mode. Output: passive event view.
language/event/incorporate.sle
Represents semantic incorporation of a noun-like role into an event concept. Required: proposition theme. Optional: mode context. Output: incorporated event.
language/event/serialise.sle
Represents a tightly linked sequence of events sharing participants or temporal structure. Required: proposition proposition. Optional: agent theme order time context. Output: serial-event structure.
language/time/locate.sle
Locates an event, state, claim, or rule at an instant or interval. Required: target time. Optional: context valid-time transaction-time remoteness. Output: temporal proposition.
language/time/before.sle
Orders one temporal entity strictly before another. Required: source target. Optional: context basis. Output: temporal relation.
language/time/after.sle
Orders one temporal entity strictly after another. Required: source target. Optional: context basis. Output: temporal relation.
language/time/overlap.sle
States that two intervals overlap under an interval semantics. Required: source target. Optional: degree context. Output: temporal relation.
language/time/during.sle
Places one event or interval wholly or partly inside another. Required: source target. Optional: mode context. Output: temporal relation.
language/time/begin.sle
Marks the inception boundary of a state, process, or validity interval. Required: target start. Optional: context evidence-mode. Output: boundary event.
language/time/end.sle
Marks the termination boundary of a state, process, or validity interval. Required: target end. Optional: context evidence-mode. Output: boundary event.
language/time/continue.sle
States temporal continuation across an interval without requiring material identity. Required: target interval. Optional: identity-mode context evidence-mode. Output: continuation proposition.
language/time/habitual.sle
Classifies a proposition as characteristically repeated rather than a single occurrence. Required: proposition. Optional: frequency interval context. Output: habitual proposition.
language/time/progressive.sle
Presents an event from within its unfolding, without asserting completion. Required: proposition. Optional: time context. Output: progressive proposition.
language/time/perfective.sle
Presents an event as a bounded whole under a named viewpoint. Required: proposition. Optional: time context. Output: perfective proposition.
language/time/imperfective.sle
Presents internal duration, habit, or incompleteness without a closed boundary. Required: proposition. Optional: time aspect context. Output: imperfective proposition.
language/time/perfect.sle
Relates a prior event or state to a later reference time through current relevance. Required: proposition time. Optional: anchor context. Output: perfect proposition.
language/time/prospective.sle
Represents a future-oriented event relative to a reference state. Required: proposition time. Optional: anchor confidence context. Output: prospective proposition.
language/time/remote-past.sle
Places a proposition in a culturally or operationally remote past category. Required: proposition. Optional: time remoteness context. Output: remote-past proposition.
language/time/recent-past.sle
Places a proposition in a recent-past category. Required: proposition. Optional: time remoteness context. Output: recent-past proposition.
language/time/near-future.sle
Places a proposition in a near-future category. Required: proposition. Optional: time remoteness confidence context. Output: near-future proposition.
language/time/remote-future.sle
Places a proposition in a remote-future category. Required: proposition. Optional: time remoteness confidence context. Output: remote-future proposition.
language/time/iterative.sle
Represents discrete repetition of a proposition. Required: proposition cardinality. Optional: frequency interval context. Output: iterative proposition.
language/time/durative.sle
Marks extended duration as semantically relevant. Required: proposition duration. Optional: interval context. Output: durative proposition.
language/time/punctual.sle
Marks an event as point-like relative to the chosen scale. Required: proposition time. Optional: scale context. Output: punctual proposition.
language/time/telic.sle
Marks an event as oriented toward an inherent endpoint. Required: proposition target. Optional: time context. Output: telic proposition.
language/time/atelic.sle
Marks an event as lacking an inherent endpoint under the selected description. Required: proposition. Optional: time context. Output: atelic proposition.
knowledge/evidence/observe.sle
Links a proposition to direct visual or multimodal observation. Required: proposition source-agent. Optional: time location source-span confidence context. Output: observational evidence.
knowledge/evidence/sense.sle
Links a proposition to direct sensory evidence with an explicit sensory mode. Required: proposition source-agent mode. Optional: time location confidence context. Output: sensory evidence.
knowledge/evidence/measure.sle
Links a proposition or value to an instrumented measurement. Required: proposition source-agent instrument value unit. Optional: uncertainty method time dataset source-span confidence context. Output: measurement evidence.
knowledge/evidence/report.sle
Links a proposition to testimony, publication, message, or other report. Required: proposition source. Optional: source-agent source-span source-depth time confidence context. Output: report evidence.
knowledge/evidence/quote.sle
Links a proposition to exact or controlled quoted language. Required: proposition source source-span. Optional: speaker addressee language time context. Output: quotation evidence.
knowledge/evidence/infer.sle
Links a proposition to an abductive, statistical, or defeasible inference from premises. Required: proposition premise. Optional: method model confidence context. Output: inferential evidence.
knowledge/evidence/deduce.sle
Links a proposition to a deductive derivation and optional proof. Required: proposition premise. Optional: rule proof profile context. Output: deductive evidence.
knowledge/evidence/abduce.sle
Records a best-explanation hypothesis rather than an established consequence. Required: proposition premise. Optional: model alternatives confidence context. Output: abductive evidence.
knowledge/evidence/remember.sle
Links a proposition to personal or institutional memory with time and confidence. Required: proposition source-agent. Optional: time source-depth confidence context. Output: memory evidence.
knowledge/evidence/simulate.sle
Links a proposition to a simulation run and its model assumptions. Required: proposition model. Optional: code environment dataset method uncertainty context. Output: simulation evidence.
knowledge/evidence/generate.sle
Links content to a generative model or algorithm without treating generation as truth. Required: proposition source-agent model. Optional: method confidence source-span context. Output: generated evidence status.
knowledge/evidence/trace.sle
Links a proposition to an executable trace, log, or provenance chain. Required: proposition source. Optional: path content-digest environment time context. Output: trace evidence.
knowledge/epistemic/know.sle
Attributes knowledge to a holder under a named epistemic profile. Required: holder proposition. Optional: basis source time context. Output: knowledge attitude.
knowledge/epistemic/believe.sle
Attributes a belief without asserting its content in the enclosing context. Required: holder proposition. Optional: confidence basis time context. Output: belief attitude.
knowledge/epistemic/suspect.sle
Attributes a low-commitment belief or suspicion. Required: holder proposition. Optional: confidence basis time context. Output: suspicion attitude.
knowledge/epistemic/doubt.sle
Attributes doubt toward a proposition. Required: holder proposition. Optional: degree basis time context. Output: doubt attitude.
knowledge/epistemic/suspend.sle
Records suspension of judgement between competing propositions. Required: holder proposition. Optional: alternative basis time context. Output: suspended attitude.
knowledge/epistemic/revise.sle
Records a change in epistemic commitment with explicit earlier and later states. Required: holder target replacement. Optional: basis time context. Output: belief-revision event.
knowledge/epistemic/conflict.sle
Records mutually incompatible supported propositions without explosion. Required: proposition proposition. Optional: source context severity. Output: epistemic conflict.
knowledge/epistemic/unknown.sle
States that a value or proposition is not known under current coverage. Required: target. Optional: missing-reason coverage context. Output: unknown status.
knowledge/epistemic/unknowable.sle
States principled or model-relative non-knowability with explicit basis. Required: target basis. Optional: profile context. Output: unknowability claim.
knowledge/epistemic/discover.sle
Records transition from unknown or unaccepted to supported. Required: holder proposition. Optional: evidence-mode time source context. Output: discovery event.
knowledge/epistemic/expect.sle
Records an expectation before evidence arrives. Required: holder proposition. Optional: probability confidence model time context. Output: expectation attitude.
knowledge/epistemic/counterexpect.sle
Marks evidence as contrary to an explicit prior expectation. Required: holder proposition premise. Optional: degree time context. Output: counterexpectation relation.
knowledge/epistemic/surprise.sle
Marks information as producing a model-relevant expectation update. Required: holder proposition. Optional: mirativity degree model time context. Output: surprise event.
knowledge/modality/possible.sle
Places a proposition in at least one admissible world or interpretation. Required: proposition context. Optional: world basis profile. Output: possibility proposition.
knowledge/modality/necessary.sle
Places a proposition in every admissible world or interpretation under a profile. Required: proposition context. Optional: world coverage basis profile. Output: necessity proposition.
knowledge/modality/impossible.sle
States that no admissible world or interpretation satisfies a proposition. Required: proposition context. Optional: coverage basis profile. Output: impossibility proposition.
knowledge/modality/contingent.sle
States that a proposition holds in some but not all admissible worlds. Required: proposition context. Optional: coverage profile. Output: contingency proposition.
knowledge/modality/counterfactual.sle
Creates a context contrary to accepted actuality and records its antecedent. Required: proposition context. Optional: assumption world basis. Output: counterfactual proposition.
knowledge/modality/realis.sle
Marks a proposition as presented as actual or realised in the named context. Required: proposition context. Optional: time evidence-mode. Output: realis proposition.
knowledge/modality/irrealis.sle
Marks a proposition as intended, desired, hypothetical, conditional, or otherwise non-actual. Required: proposition context. Optional: mode time holder. Output: irrealis proposition.
social/deontic/require.sle
Creates an obligation with holder, authority, conditions, and validity. Required: holder proposition authority. Optional: when unless jurisdiction valid-time severity context. Output: obligation.
social/deontic/permit.sle
